**Valen reveals that Ilvara communes with a giant pulsing fungal mass she believes is Lolth, emerging with new terrifying spores and fanatical devotion to an entity she calls "the bride," whom the party knows to be Zuggtmoy.**

*A chilling revelation confirming that the Demon Queen Zuggtmoy is actively corrupting and manipulating Ilvara's religious devotion.*

## Chapter 050

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/050-session-fungus-among-us.md`; chapter 50; section Memorable Moments

**Glabbagool attempts to disguise himself as an inanimate rock, but cannot stop his eyes from blinking and looking left and right, completely undermining the illusion.**

*The party needed to stay hidden while Daz performed his ruse for Asha Vandree; Glabbagool's attempt at stealth was endearingly terrible.*

> "Then he starts talking and has to be reminded that, you know, Invisible Rock doesn't talk."
> — GM as Narrator

> "Eyes that will look left and right are not very invisible."
> — GM as Narrator

> "Are we talking about the game, or just how you live? Either way."
> — Zalthir's player

> "It's taken me a while to admit this, but I have control issues."
> — GM as Narrator

*Jokingly explaining why he is forever the GM, after being teased about his world-building decisions.*

**Daz's "possession" performance.**

> "Asha. Dear servant."
> — Daz

> "You're a wizard. A wizard… a wizard who prays to Lolth?"
> — Asha Vandree

> "There are many imitations, but as you saw, I came to you in my smaller form, and led you here. You should know the truth."
> — Daz

**Asha's paranoid misread of the failed deception.**

> "Aha, you are trying to test me, to see if I was one of those fake followers of Lolth, who are willing to believe every charlatan magic user that is somehow being possessed by the power of Lolth."
> — Asha Vandree

> "Yeah, yeah, okay, hold on. I can't roll three fives."
> — Daz's player, after spending his inspiration on the reroll

> "Is this another trick of Ilvara's?"
> — Asha Vandree

> "Perhaps my prayers have been answered through this… confused vessel."
> — Asha Vandree

**Asha calls for blood.**

> "We need to kill her. We need to butcher her! We need to send her to an unearthly grave! I fear that her disease will spread."
> — Asha Vandree

**The price of an assassination.**

> "The gratitude of Lolth?"
> — Asha Vandree

> "I already feel the gratitude of Lolth. I have that."
> — Daz

**Conviction, of a sort.**

> "That is not the kind of conviction a follower of Lolth shows."
> — Asha Vandree

> "It's the kind of conviction this follower of Lolth shows."
> — Daz *(tape reads "law"; Lolth per the GM's preceding line)*

**Asha calls Zalthir Daz's "servant," and Zalthir calls Daz his "boss."**

> "Your tactical awareness, Zalthir, is to be commended. … You have found a great servant, Daz."
> — Asha Vandree

> "Boss? I thought he was your master. Very well. Boss it is!"
> — Asha Vandree

**Asha is fed up with the exes.**

> "It's very awkward, really, to be stuck with these two. I mean, they used to have an affair, it used to be this whole thing, and then she broke up with him, and then he was bitter because she broke up with him, and now I've had to be in the middle of these two for the last few months. Do you have any idea how aggravating that is?"
> — Asha Vandree

**Ilvara melts into a fungal patch and reappears elsewhere, screaming in fury, just as Zalthir attempts to fly her over the heart fungus to drop her into the area of effect.**

*Zalthir had successfully grappled Ilvara and was carrying her toward the heart fungus when she used her legendary action to escape.*

> "I mean, that thing is all heart."
> — Gyrgum, on the Heart Fungus lashing out

**Thorin lures Jorlan directly into Gyrgum's pre-set Glyph of Warding by taunting him, triggering a massive explosion that damages Jorlan and several sporewalkers simultaneously.**

*Nobody expected the taunt to work so cleanly; the trap detonated perfectly, turning the tide of the opening round of combat.*

> "come get it, idiot."
> — Thorin

> "Kaboom! I didn't expect it to work."
> — Gyrgum

*The moment Jorlan charged directly onto his explosive Glyph of Warding.*

> "That worked better than I had any right to expect."
> — Gyrgum

## Chapter 051

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/051-session-a-spore-filled-finale.md`; chapter 51; section Memorable Moments

**Zalthir grapples Ilvara mid-air, delivers two critical strikes while holding her aloft, then drops her forty feet to the ground — burning her legendary action in the process.**

*A daring aerial assault that turned Ilvara's own mobility against her and set the tone for the entire final battle.*

> “And then I dropped.”
> — Zalthir

> “Oh my gosh, Zalthir, you made a mistake.”
> — GM as Narrator, after Zalthir briefly miscalculated his damage

> “How many pistachios did you waste on that?”
> — Gyrgum

**Thorin destroys the heart fungus in a single round of strikes from Dawnbringer, his sentient sun-blade, cutting short an elaborate encounter the DM had spent considerable time preparing.**

*The fungus was described as resistant to the usual things that affect fungi but “doubly not resistant” to radiant damage. Thorin exploited that weakness immediately.*

> “It's not resistant to swords.”
> — Gyrgum

> “Boy, I had so much prepared for the fungus. Oh well.”
> — Gyrgum

> “I do what I can.”
> — Thorin

**Gyrgum's Guiding Bolt strikes Ilvara and her vulnerability to radiant energy causes her to explode in a cloud of poisonous spores, ending the battle but engulfing nearby allies.**

*The killing blow was both triumphant and dangerous, as the toxic aftermath caught Zalthir and Thorin in its radius.*

> “She is particularly vulnerable to radiant damage, so that actually causes her to die. She explodes in a puff of poisonous spores.”
> — GM as Narrator

> “Oh, boy.”
> — Zalthir

> “Not ideal. How big is the cloud?”
> — Gyrgum

> “I'm glad I was standing back.”
> — Gyrgum

> “Can I kill your brother?”
> — Kaelira Duskryn

*Asked of Nym Duskryn after Daz refused to stand down from combat, prompting Nym Duskryn to dryly note her brother was always the dumber one.*

> “I am not gonna leave. Everyone here… to fight for me without me also participating. I don't give a damn if you get paid. The best way for you to get paid, then, is to continue the fight and prevent all of us from dying.”
> — Daz

> “We will meet again. Enjoy your victory for the moment.”
> — House T'sarran warrior

*The surviving drow elite warrior's parting threat before retreating into the tunnels.*

> “Come with me if you want to live, you idiot.”
> — Kaelira Duskryn

*The rescue was less polished than the famous line it echoed, but Kaelira crossed Ilvara's insect plague and suffered for it to pull Daz clear.*

> “You know what a monk does. You run, run, run, and then slaps.”
> — GM as Narrator

> “Pretty much!”
> — Zalthir

## Chapter 052

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/052-session-the-ember-grapple.md`; chapter 52; section Memorable Moments

**The GM announces the level-up after a long pregame conversation, catching the party completely by surprise.**

> “Alright, so the first thing is that you're all level 8 now.”
> — GM

> “You buried the lead, GM.”
> — Daz

> “Alright, well, that's gonna take the rest of the hour.”
> — Thorin

**Thorin asks Zalthir whether he builds balanced characters or simply embraces extreme specialization.**

> “I find it more fun to double down with some sort of obscure specialization and then try to break the universe with it.”
> — Zalthir

This became the table's working definition of the “Zalthir theory of micro-optimization.”

**The GM explains why D&D rewards specialization while still demanding that the party cover one another's weaknesses.**

> “At the 20th level, you better be doing 70 points of damage per round.”
> — GM

> “The downside is, if you're facing a dragon and the entire party has one guy who can shoot things from a remote distance, the dragon stands at about 60 feet and breathes on you, and that's the end of the party.”
> — GM

**Thorin consults an AI to help choose his level-up feat, only for it to first recommend Sharpshooter and then immediately advise against it — before the GM steps in with a house rule to make it work anyway.**

> “So, Gemini is… it convinced me to do Sharpshooter, and now it's telling me not to, and I should focus on strength.”
> — Thorin

> “So, can I do sharpshooter and actually put the plus one into Strength instead of Dex?”
> — Thorin

> “Sure.”
> — GM

At the end of the session, the GM admitted that Gemini's proposed “common quality of life's house rule” might itself have been invented:

> “I'm curious to know whether that was a hallucination on the house rule, but I'm okay with it.”
> — GM

**Daz recounts the fastest of his three jury-duty experiences, in which a cab driver denied responsibility after backing over someone despite clear video evidence.**

> “And one of the ladies across the way goes, it sounds like you're the foreperson. And I said, okay. And then they said, is everybody here thinking guilty? We were like, yep.”
> — Daz *(table chatter — out of character)*

> “maybe you shouldn't be driving.”
> — Daz *(table chatter — out of character)*

The jury returned its verdict in roughly seven and a half minutes.

**Zalthir discovers the Street Justice feat, unlocking the ability to bind grappled enemies with rope — and throwing his original plan into chaos.**

> “I'm down a whole rabbit hole, because I only just learned that it is possible to restrain using Rope, and there's an associated feat for that.”
> — Zalthir

> “I saw that feat, and I'm like, oh, Zalthir's gonna love this one.”
> — Thorin

> “I don't know why you wouldn't pick this.”
> — GM

> “Yeah, the only rea… so, I was planning the… just the normal plus 2 to Dex. Now my whole plan is thrown into chaos.”
> — Zalthir

**The party recognizes the synergy between Zalthir's grappling and Daz's Cloud of Daggers.**

> “If you've grappled them, they get hit because I cast it. They also get hit at the end of the turn.”
> — Daz

> “I mean, I will happily just drag them in and out repeatedly, depending on how many times GM will let them take the damage.”
> — Zalthir

> “Just to be clear, it is one per turn.”
> — GM

**The GM invokes his authority against any supposed infinite-damage interpretation.**

> “And I said, I don't care what the rules say, there's not an infinite damage hack.”
> — GM

> “That's fair.”
> — Zalthir

**The party jokingly proposes renaming their adventuring group the “Ember Grapple” after Zalthir's new Street Justice feat turns their combat strategy into a grapple-and-stab operation.**

> “maybe the Ember Vanguard will be known as the Ember Grapple.”
> — GM

> “Seems appropriate.”
> — Zalthir

**After the entire session is consumed by level-eight choices, the GM declares the party ready to play—next week.**

> “Okay, cool, so we're good to… we're good to play next week, is what I'm hearing. Awesome.”
> — GM

## Chapter 053

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/053-session-out-of-the-dark-and-into-the-darkness.md`; chapter 53; section Memorable Moments

> “When attacking a cleric of Lolth, one does not just simply grapple them. One takes your blade and cuts their heads off.”
> — Nym

*Exasperated after watching Thorin grapple and gag Asha Vandree instead of killing her outright.*

> “If Daz can't protect himself from a low-level cult initiate of Lolth, then, quite frankly, whoever paid for his survival deserves to lose his money.”
> — Nym and Kaelira Duskryn

*Dismissively explaining why the mercenaries felt no obligation to protect Daz from Asha Vandree.*

> “Our job was not to protect you from evil demon gods, our job was to protect you from two very specific threats.”
> — Nym and Kaelira Duskryn

*Clarifying the exact and decidedly limited scope of their contract.*

> “Excellent! Now that I am in charge!”
> — Asha Vandree

*Asha's first attempt to assert command after convincing herself that her survival proved she had been divinely chosen.*

> “I am blessed by Lolth!”
> — Asha Vandree

*Triumphantly responding when Gyrgum's guiding bolt missed her moments before Zalthir killed her.*

> “Well done, well done, well done!”
> — Kaelira Duskryn

*Applauding after Zalthir's flurry of attacks abruptly ended Asha's imagined reign.*

> “The mage Daz, and confirms the extraction is alive, unharmed.”
> — the GM, reading the burned orders

*The surviving portion of the extraction instructions, confirming that House T'sarran wanted Daz taken alive.*

> “Why has everybody focused on me?”
> — Daz

*The central mystery confronting the party after discovering that one expensive contract protected Daz while another sought to abduct him.*

> “So then maybe there's more to me than meets the eye?”
> — Daz

*Daz considering whether his apparently unremarkable background might conceal something important.*

> “Yes, yes, yes, that's what every orphan thinks.”
> — Kaelira Duskryn

*Kaelira puncturing Daz's speculation about a mysterious heritage.*

> “I'm not afraid of the dark, I just don't like the dark.”
> — Dawnbringer

*Dawnbringer firmly distinguishing personal preference from fear when Thorin suggested helping her overcome her trauma.*

> “You know what would help? … To audit their books.”
> — Daz

*Daz returning to his favored investigative method, prompting the party's now-customary groan.*

**After months trapped in the Underdark, the party finally crawls into the blinding light of the surface world — the Overbright — with Glabbagool peering out from a bag of holding, seeing the sun for the first time.**

*A triumphant end to the party's long underground ordeal and the beginning of a new mystery on the surface.*

## Chapter 054

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/054-session-04-28.md`; chapter 54; section Memorable Moments

> "Giant friend, we are like brothers. We have fought together. We have bled together. We have faced the abyssal creatures together."
> — Eldeth

*Eldeth's heartfelt farewell to Thorin, which the entire table expected to end with "but you can't come to Gauntlgrym yet" — and which, to everyone's surprise, did not*

> "I'm taking this seriously. I don't think you're just being socially nice here. I think you really do want me to."
> — Thorin

*Thorin making absolutely sure Eldeth understood he intended to hold her to her promise*

**Gyrgum rolls a 9 on his Insight check and confidently concludes that the mysterious entity stalking the party through the forest is a deer.**

*Especially funny because, as the Overbright is where he is from, Gyrgum genuinely does know what a deer looks like — he just rolled that badly*

> "Do not read the word assassin. That's just the profile of the character."
> — GM

*The GM hastily reassuring the players after accidentally displaying the drow tracker's character sheet label on screen*

**Gyrgum casually offers the drow spy a job, leaving her visibly confused and uncertain whether she is actually going to survive the encounter.**

*The most disarming interrogation tactic in the party's repertoire: genuine friendliness toward someone who expected to die*

**Gyrgum hands the drow spy the prayer beads of Bahamut without a word of explicit threat and tells her to put them on, causing her to visibly panic at the religious implications.**

*A completely silent intimidation that required no roll — just the presentation of a holy item to a drow cultist who had no idea what the rituals of Bahamut actually entailed, and feared the worst*

> "So basically, you're treating me as bait. You're watching and observing so that when something happens to me, you can go report back what happened to me."
> — Daz

*Daz arriving at the deeply uncomfortable realization that House T'sarran does not actually care about him at all*

> "My matron believes the one who paid for him will reveal themselves once he reaches the surface."
> — Drow Spy

*The spy's revelation that House T'sarran is using Daz as bait to identify a powerful, unknown benefactor — a mystery that now concerns both the party and their captive equally*

**The party uses magical shrinking mushrooms to reduce the drow spy to a tiny size, nicknames her "Polly Pocket," stores her in a bag of holding with Thorin proposing holes poked in it "like a hamster," and assigns Glabbagool as her guard.**

*The party's creative and deeply undignified solution to transporting their captive, complete with Thorin offering the small mercy that at least the bag of holding provides shade from the sun*

> "Tiny, tiny little vengeance."
> — Daz

*Daz's response to the shrunken spy declaring that House T'sarran would have its vengeance, after she had just been reduced to pocket size and placed in a bag with a grey ooze*

**Glabbagool intimidates the shrunken drow prisoner by suddenly manifesting a large number of eyes all at once, making the situation deeply unsettling.**

*Glabbagool asserting his role as prison guard in the most uniquely ooze-like way possible, after having already informed the spy that his googly eyes are affectations and he can actually see many different ways*

> "This is all novel news! I can't wait to take advantage of it! The field of Bahamut orc conversions will benefit tremendously from your interview."
> — Vareth

*The quintessential niche academic finally getting his moment in the sun — his previously ignored research has become the hottest field in academia, conference invitations and funding requests are pouring in, and a living orc cleric of Bahamut just walked through his door*

## Chapter 055

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/055-session-the-ember-grapple-s-journey-through-the-north.md`; chapter 55; section Memorable Moments

> "One day they're going to ban oozes. The next day they'll ban sentient swords!"
> — Dawnbringer

*The sentient sword chiming in during Thorin's impassioned public argument for ooze rights as a city guard tried to inspect Glabbagool's bag in Waterdeep*

> "This is very good."
> — Glabbagool, on cheese

*The intelligent grey ooze, gifted an entire wheel of cheese by a curious innkeeper in Triboar, dissolves it entirely — then asks with genuine curiosity whether dairy is bad for people. His first experience with cheese.*

> "The kraken under the keep, the kraken under the keep. The chant will break and the kraken will rise."
> — The Field Ward Street Preacher

*A ragged street preacher in Waterdeep's Field Ward delivering a cryptic repeating chant, then pausing to recognize Daz as a drow and warn him directly: "You came up! You came up, you will go again. You will go down again. Do not—" before cycling back into the chant*

> "That's Eldred. He ran courier, Triboar to Sundabar in the war, came back from his last run two summers past, and he was both of them. We feed him, and we don't ask."
> — A neighbor at the Triboar inn

*The party realizes the madness of the Underdark is bleeding into the surface world, as a local courier has returned from his last run speaking in two voices — his own and one that answers questions he never asked*

> "Are we not all the playthings of fate?"
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum's response to Bahamutian clerics in Triboar demanding to know where he stands on "the only question of the age" — accidentally winning over the Bahamutian faction while causing the Stroudites and Protanthians to storm out*

**Thorin launches into a passionate public speech defending ooze rights while Zalthir quietly teleports away with Glabbagool's bag, leaving a bewildered city guard staring at the vanished bag and a dwarf mid-oration — who then finishes his coffee and walks away.**

*A city guard in Waterdeep insisted on inspecting the bag containing Glabbagool, triggering an impromptu civil rights stand*

**Daz walks into Rishaal's Pageturners and discovers that Whistler is believed by some factions to have died at Glazhael's lair, with a memorial sign reading "Whistler, Kenku of the First Faction, fallen at Glazhael's lair… May his silence speak."**

*Daz browsing a Waterdeep bookshop and stumbling upon the legend that has grown around the Kenku hero Whistler*

> "That painting saved the print run! Milo finished it two days before the signing. We had it framed. People come here for the painting and left with a book."
> — Rishaal

*The proprietor of Rishaal's Pageturners explaining how Milo Goodbarrel's painting of the war's heroes rescued Volume Three from commercial failure*

## Chapter 056

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/056-session-candlekeep-trials-truths-and-therapy.md`; chapter 56; section Memorable Moments

> "The wells are not wells anymore."
> — Elin

*The only words spoken by the silent seven-year-old girl after Dawnbringer's lesser restoration — delivered after a pompous declaration of "Let me bring the light to you" — hinting at the Underdark madness spreading to the surface world.*

> "Let me bring the light to you."
> — Dawnbringer

*Dawnbringer takes on what the DM describes as "a very pompous voice" before casting lesser restoration on the afflicted child, only for the cure to prove incomplete.*

**Zalthir fails every skill demonstration — acrobatics, athletics, and stealth — during the Trial of the Broken Mirror, then lands a powerful hit on the departing monk and earns his respect.**

*After rolling a 3, a 1, and an 11 on formal tests, Zalthir's combat-optimized character saves face by landing a hit no one has managed in a long time — prompting the monk to say "Perhaps not all is lost."*

*Master Kenshi concedes that although Zalthir's formal skills are weak, there is something special about how he combines Shadow Step with a strike — a strength that redeems his other shortcomings.*

> "She needs to remember what daylight feels like without needing to fight, and then when she's ready and you are willing, she needs to grieve. The first work is light. The second work is mourning. We will start with the first."
> — Master Archivist Philemon

*A clinical artifact specialist reframes Dawnbringer's trauma in deeply emotional terms, turning what seemed like a weapon assessment into something closer to therapy — with Thorin noting afterward that he apparently signed them both up for it.*

> "There is one house I cannot speak of in this room, not because I will not, because the public record is sealed, and I am today the public record. If you want what is behind the seals, you will have to want it through other doors."
> — Sister Yvenne

*Yvenne reveals to Daz that someone powerful has had an entire Drow noble house erased from Candlekeep's records, hinting it may be connected to his identity.*

> "She keeps trying to kill me! And then I slap her around, and then she tries to run away, but there's nowhere to run away to, so I just sort of expand, and then she's trapped, and then she starts screaming at me, and then I try to explain to her how the way of the shadow monk is to be quiet, and that she should learn meditation, and then she screams at me about the meditation, and then we talk about things. We've become great friends!"
> — Glabbagool

*Glabbagool cheerfully describes his daily routine as jailer, which involves being attacked, expanding to trap the spy, and offering unsolicited monk wisdom — all of which Zalthir confirms is excellent pre-monk training.*

> "It is a softening name. Mothers use it for boys they wanted to live."
> — Sister Yvenne, on the name Daz'issin

*After Daz discovers a name possibly connected to his own in a footnote of a heraldry book with its appendix razored out, Yvenne's quiet explanation of what the name means lands with considerable weight.*

**Daz discovers that an entire appendix has been razored out of a 200-year-old heraldry book — cut mechanically, not worn — and a surviving footnote references "Daz'issin," possibly his own true name.**

*Piecing together censored records and a softening name used by mothers for sons they hoped would survive, Daz begins to suspect his origins are far more dangerous than he knew, pushing back: "But what if they weren't successfully all gone?"*

## Chapter 057

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/057-session-candlekeep-s-cold-case-the-gyrgumite-gambit.md`; chapter 57; section Memorable Moments

**Gyrgum proposes a fourth philosophical position — 'fixed points' around which mortal choices are constrained — sending Brother Vareth into a frenzy of academic excitement; Gyrgum later presents it to the council as the 'Gyrgumite School.'**

*During a deep theological discussion with Vareth about the Question of the Age, Gyrgum casually upended decades of established debate with a single idea.*

> "But… but what if there's a fourth? What if there are certain fixed points around which our actions must revolve? And the closer we come to one of those fixed points, the more constrained we are, but between them, perhaps we have a great deal of freedom of action."
> — Gyrgum

> "A fourth principle! Perhaps a compromise! This was not a triangle that everyone argued, but a square!"
> — Brother Vareth

**Gyrgum introduces himself to the chaotic Council of Twelve in the middle of a murder crisis.**

> "I am the head of the Gyrgumite School."
> — Gyrgum
>
> "Founder, in fact."
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum's opening line to the chaotic Council of Twelve, immediately derailing a murder crisis into an academic debate.*

**Fembris takes one look at Janussi's mutilated corpse, sprints to the nearest window, flings it open, and vomits outside the tower — the party's assigned assistant immediately sidelined by the crime scene.**

*The enthusiastic young acolyte who had been cheerfully guiding the party all morning was completely undone by the grisly reality of the murder.*

**The party discovers that Janussi's heart was forcibly removed after his death, apparently to prevent *Raise Dead*.**

*Gyrgum recognizes that `Raise Dead` requires the vital organs to be present.*

> "You can't raise the dead of somebody if the vital organs are not there."
> — Gyrgum

**The party hears a prophecy recited from the high gallery: 'On the longest night of winter's deepest cold, the keeper falls, the heart is sold' — the evening before the Keeper of Tomes was found with his heart removed.**

*The prophecy of Alaundo, heard casually at dinner, takes on chilling significance the very next morning when the murder is discovered.*

**Gatewarden Kalan Strongbranch is publicly stripped of the murder investigation in front of the full council and the party, bowing curtly before storming off — and Tadric later notes he looked more determined after his private inspection of the crime scene.**

*The dismissal raises immediate suspicion: did the Gatewarden find something in the room before the party arrived?*

**The party asks for remuneration before accepting the investigation.**

> "Honorarium? Is there an honorarium for taking on this august role and position?"
> — Thorin

*Thorin's phrasing first drew appreciative laughter; the request itself then stunned the council into silence.*

> "The Library of Candlekeep is not in the habit of giving out books."
> — Bookwyrm

> "Well, we're not in the habit for investigating for free."
> — Thorin

**Zalthir delivers a single quiet line about the mysterious gray cat that stops Fembris in his tracks.**

*Fembris had been dismissing the Avowed's reverence for the cat as overblown superstition, but Zalthir's deadpan response gave even the skeptical acolyte pause.*

> "Is it?"
> — Zalthir

## Chapter 058

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/058-session-the-case-of-the-missing-tickles.md`; chapter 58; section Memorable Moments

> "Body is dead."
> — Daz

*Daz's attempt at a Sherlock Holmes impression while examining the murder victim, after being prompted to deduce something dramatic*

**Daz earns an Inspiration point for casually referencing Milo's Murder Mystery as in-world research that informed his investigation technique.**

*Daz's literary reference delighted the DM enough to award Inspiration*

**The party realizes the figure seen leaving the tower looked like Sylvira Savikas but did not smell like her, suggesting a shapeshifter or magical disguise was used to frame or impersonate her.**

*Queenie the cat's offhand remark about smell becomes the session's most chilling revelation, pointing to a deeper conspiracy*

> "You may now leave, and please give yourself a proper licking before returning to my tower."
> — Queenie

*The cat dismissing the party after their interrogation, offended by the general hygiene standards of two-legged creatures*

**Gyrgum deduces that Janussi's nose was broken before he died — bruising cannot occur post-mortem — meaning someone assaulted him earlier in the evening before the actual murder.**

*A sharp medicine check turns a seemingly minor detail into a key piece of the timeline, narrowing the window of the attack*

**The party discovers that whoever entered the chamber left behind a fortune in diamonds, platinum, and gold — but stole only a single sapphire, a parchment, and a locket, suggesting the motive was never robbery.**

*Opening the safe leads the party to suspect a targeted, arcane purpose rather than simple theft*

**Thorin asks if he can take the candle from the fallen marble statue, causing Tadric to spend the rest of the scene nervously watching the statue, convinced it might come to life.**

*An innocent question from a battle-hardened adventurer sends the anxious attendant into a spiral of paranoia about animated statues*

> "I'm ready for the statue to come alive. That's just, that's the way this shit works."
> — Thorin

*Thorin’s blunt, battle-worn pragmatism about animated statues, delivered with deadpan seriousness, becomes a running joke that haunts Tadric*

> "To Janussi, read it and weep, you old snob, a proper Deadwinter gift, your loving rival."
> — Gift tag

*The gift tag’s bitter, sarcastic tone — “your loving rival” — becomes the emotional core of the motive, instantly framing Daral Yashenti as the prime suspect*

> "There have been too little… Tickles and too many questions."
> — Queenie

*The cat’s dismissive, haughty response to the party’s interrogation, blending feline indifference with cryptic insight*

> "I was never sure but what the keeper saw in her, but he certainly incurred her wrath that morning. Raging she was, screamed that he was a traitor!"
> — Hollypocket

*The housekeeper’s visceral description of Sylvira’s outburst, revealing the emotional volatility underlying the scholarly setting*

> "He carried it, he carried it all, all the time. He never took it off."
> — Queenie (on the locket)

*The cat’s simple, unemotional confirmation that the locket was a constant companion — making its theft even more personal and significant*

## Chapter 059

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/059-session-questions-answers-and-a-key.md`; chapter 59; section Memorable Moments

> "So many questions and so few tickles. You may leave now, and please give yourself a proper licking before returning to my tower."
> — Queenie

*Recounted by the DM as Queenie's dismissal of the party at the end of the previous session, setting the comedic tone for the evening*

> "Whatever Bookwyrm is telling you, listen for what she isn't. The High Tower has two keys. Janussi wore one, I held the other. I have given it now to someone who is not me. If I and I do not see you tomorrow, do not look for me at my post."
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*Pressed quietly into Gyrgum's hand in the corridor, eyes checking left and right before he spoke — a clandestine handoff in which Kalan presented the brass key as genuine and said he feared for his life*

> "I believe you're only being employed so you're likely to bungle the case, or else be silent if you do happen upon the right scent. You don't matter. You're outsiders."
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*Kalan's blunt opening assessment of why the party had been hired — delivered before he reluctantly agreed to collaborate after the party corrected one of his assumptions*

> "Why don't we all go off and do offline chat… have offline meetings, and come back and have a meeting where we go over all of our findings, you know, in a group setting where we'll examine everything one by one."
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*Kalan proposes a collaboration framework in strikingly corporate terms; Gyrgum asks, "Can we divide this into work streams?" Kalan proposes five, and the exchange ends with a mutual commitment to "merge all the things"*

> "Oh, look, it's Thorin's most famous detectives — Bookwyrm's Brown-Nosers!"
> — Daral Yashenti

*Daral Yashenti's mocking greeting to the party upon their arrival at the Hearth, drunk and in high spirits after Janussi's death*

**Kalan secretly presses a brass key he presents as a High Tower key into Gyrgum's hand, warning that if he is killed, it must not be found on him.**

*A tense, clandestine handoff that the party interprets as evidence of Kalan's fear and trust*

**Daral Yashenti is found at the Hearth cheerfully reading the very book suspected of being laced with midnight tears poison, licking his fingers as he turns the pages.**

*The party realizes in real time that Daral Yashenti may have poisoned himself without knowing it, raising the stakes of the interrogation dramatically*

> "You've got to do something!"
> — Daral Yashenti

*Daral Yashenti's panicked reaction after the party reveals the book he has been happily reading and licking his fingers through is likely coated in a lethal poison*

> "There's no telling what's going on in her lizard brain."
> — Daral Yashenti

*Daral's disparaging remark while naming Bookwyrm as a possible suspect, delivered with casual reptilian prejudice — and immediately challenged by a party member standing up on behalf of lizardfolk*

> "Glabbagool-Zalthir combo action."
> — Glabbagool

*Glabbagool's triumphant declaration as he attaches himself to Zalthir's arm after completing his philosophical trial at the Whispering Dome*

> "The body that mimics nothing is already shaped by what it watches."
> — Glabbagool

*Glabbagool's surprisingly profound answer to his own contemplation about the art of movement, formalizing his apprenticeship and sidekick role*

## Chapter 060

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/060-session-never-mind-the-murders.md`; chapter 60; section Memorable Moments

**Thorin looks into the lead-lined chalice of Milil and finds a severed heart and a blood-stained meat cleaver, which the party takes as the killer’s attempt to stop anyone resurrecting Janussi and asking what happened.**

*Daral had just remembered that the chalice’s lead lining would block divination. Thorin ordered him to stand back, warning that Dawnbringer might not protect him if the search went badly.*

> “And you find a heart, a heart, and a blood-stained meat cleaver.”
> — GM as Narrator

> “He left it in San Francisco.”
> — Thorin

*Thorin’s deadpan quip broke the tension immediately after the gruesome discovery.*

**Daz bribes a squirrel named Nibbles with nuts to obtain eyewitness testimony about the theft of dangerous toxins from the apothecary.**

*Daz carefully withheld each new nut until Nibbles supplied another piece of information, eventually uncovering the coordinated actions of A’lai and a Dragonborn.*

> “Oh, you’ve got nuts.”
> — Nibbles

> “I know more if you have more nuts.”
> — Nibbles

> “And I show him a nut, but I don’t give it to him until he tells me who he saw.”
> — Daz

**Daz pauses to acknowledge the absurdity of conducting a murder investigation through a squirrel witness.**

> “So I’m questioning a squirrel.”
> — Daz

> “He goes, yes, you’re questioning a squirrel.”
> — GM as Narrator

> “Just want to make sure we’re all in the state of what’s happening right now.”
> — Daz

**Zalthir discovers a copy of *1001 Tashalian Nights* in Alkrist’s room, bookmarked to a tale about a commander poisoned by Midnight Tears through a gift—the method apparently used to kill Janussi.**

*The book lay on Alkrist’s bedside table near sealed jars of dead rats with blistered, pustulated skin.*

> “Okay, that’s crazy suspicious.”
> — Zalthir

**The party realizes that the figure seen in the kitchens at 1 a.m. carrying a lantern and refusing to speak may not have been Sylvira Savikas at all.**

*The figure merely pointed at her throat, avoided speaking in Sylvira’s voice, and used a light despite appearing to be a tiefling.*

> “That was actually her, somebody wearing her face, right? Because they didn’t talk.”
> — Thorin

> “And she was using a lantern, even though she’s a tiefling.”
> — GM as Narrator

**Thorin weighs Daral’s life against the evidentiary value of watching the poison run its course.**

> “Look, I don’t want to kill the guy. But also, there’s vital information at stake here.”
> — Thorin

*Daral’s rapidly sobering expression made clear that the people he regarded as his only protectors were still debating whether his death might provide useful evidence.*

**Daral proposes magically sealing gift wrapping as Candlekeep’s newest institutional safety practice.**

> “Why would I put a magical seal on the wrapping? It’s a gift. Nobody. This is Candlekeep.”
> — Daral

> “If I make it through the night, hint, hint, hint, I will be sure to tell everyone that we must practice that glyph of warding for everyone.”
> — Daral

**Glabbagool enthusiastically attempts stealth after learning silence.**

> “I have learnt silence.”
> — Glabbagool

> “Am I being silent enough? Was this good silence or was it too loud silence?”
> — Glabbagool

> “Please hear my silence.”
> — Thorin

**Thorin summarizes the viciousness of Candlekeep’s academic rivalries.**

> “You know what they say? The fights are so vicious because the stakes are so low.”
> — Thorin

## Chapter 061

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/061-session-the-colloquium-was-well-received.md`; chapter 61; section Memorable Moments

> “I think Thorin is a fantastic investigator, an absolutely astonishing investigator. And his charming partner, Dawnbringer, is a stunning, absolutely stunning…”
> — Daral

*Believing he has poisoned himself and needs Lesser Restoration, Daral praises everyone he thinks might help him.*

> “Oh, and in due time, my dear. In due time.”
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum confirms that he can save Daral, but sees no reason to hurry while Daral remains useful to the investigation.*

> “Please deposit them with my secretary.”
> — Gyrgum

> “This is not nutritious.”
>
> “This is not food.”
>
> “I remember when there was cheese. Perhaps if we had some cheese.”
> — Glabbagool

*Gyrgum delegates Fembris’s eagerly prepared research notes to Glabbagool, who evaluates them according to the standards of an ooze.*

> “I think you might misunderstand the term monograph. It’s a work that has one author.”
> — Gyrgum

> “A duograph, perhaps then.”
> — Fembris Lancer

*Fembris rapidly revises his hoped-for academic collaboration after learning that Gyrgum has no intention of sharing authorship.*

> “Walls don’t stop us. I can eat through walls.”
> — Glabbagool

*Reflecting on Alkrist’s locked safe, Glabbagool recognizes that the party’s infiltration options were less constrained than they had assumed.*

> “Magic does not make evidence meaningful. It makes evidence adversarial.”
> — Elian

*Elian articulates the central argument of Kalan Strongbranch’s investigative methodology: magical evidence still requires corroboration.*

> “Once you have eliminated the impossible, whatever remains, however unlikely, must be the truth.”
> — Gyrgum

> “Can I use that?”
> — Elian

> “Yes, I made it up myself.”
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum contributes a suspiciously familiar maxim to the emerging field of Faerûnian criminal investigation.*

> “What do you mean I’ve been framed? … This is outrageous. Are you here to accuse me of a crime?”
> — Sylvira

> “I believe I said you were framed.”
> — Daz

*Sylvira’s fear and outrage collide with Daz’s insistence that he is warning her, not accusing her.*

> “Honey? I would never drink honey. What kind of nonsense is this?”
> — Sylvira

*Sylvira categorically rejects one of the reported actions of the person seen wearing her likeness.*

> “We were discussing how to deal with our mutual problem of Janussi.”
> — Alkrist

*Under the Zone of Truth, Alkrist finally describes the purpose of his private meeting with A'lai at the baths.*

> “The part he bookmarked. Is that right? So I can just look.”
> — Zalthir

*Zalthir dramatically opens Alkrist’s copy of* 1001 Tashalian Nights *to the Midnight Tears passage that Alkrist used as his murder manual.*

> “I didn’t actually kill him. I just, you know, put some poison on a book.”
> — Alkrist

*Alkrist attempts to draw a legal distinction between poisoning Janussi’s book and killing Janussi.*

> “Yes, I’m under mind control right now. All of these things I’ve said, they’re so true and yet so true.”
> — Alkrist

*Unable to deny his confession under the Zone of Truth, Alkrist tries to recast truthful testimony as magical coercion.*

> “Ripped out his heart? What? His heart was ripped out?”
> — Alkrist

*Alkrist’s genuine shock reveals that poisoning Janussi and mutilating his corpse were separate parts of the conspiracy.*

## Chapter 062

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/062-session-academic-vindication-and-an-assault.md`; chapter 62; section Memorable Moments

> “I mean, I was ready to murder somebody at the end of every performance season when I was at Google for what it's worth.”
> — Thorin

*Reacting to the idea that Janussi's habit of giving bad reviews may have motivated his murder, drawing a relatable real-world parallel.*

> “I mean, I thought so too, Dawnbringer. I don't know what to tell you.”
> — Thorin

*Sympathizing with Dawnbringer's disappointment that the promised therapy sessions with Candlekeep's scholars had not continued.*

> “I mean, I'm not leaving here about the therapy. Like, that first session was very productive, actually. I really feel that we need to.”
> — Dawnbringer

*Dawnbringer makes it clear that the interrupted scholarly therapy remains an important personal objective.*

> “You have a confession, a heart, and a cleaver. The confession is one spell, the weakest channel by the standard I wrote. The heart on the cleaver proves someone covered this up cleverly. Neither says who it was.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*Kalan applies his Threefold Proof methodology even when doing so makes the party's apparent victory considerably less convenient.*

> “Sometimes the clever play is to be patient.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*Kalan argues that Candlekeep's approaching naming ceremony can politically isolate Bookwyrm before the party risks a direct confrontation.*

> “Yes, but then he says the ultimate academic thing: if she is removed from her position as an avowed, that would be far more damning than any crime we could convict her of.”
> — DM

*For Kalan, academic and institutional disgrace may be a harsher punishment than a conventional criminal sentence.*

> “One more thing.”
> — Thorin

*Thorin imagines returning after Bookwyrm believes she has escaped scrutiny, armed with the book obtained through the party's reward.*

**Zalthir uses Glabbagool, fused to his arm, to secretly absorb Bookwyrm's offered tea, with only a faint singe of acid betraying the ruse.**

*Refusing to trust Bookwyrm's tea but not wanting to expose his suspicions, Zalthir whispers that this should be “a moment of quiet reflection” with no loud noises from Glabbagool.*

> “You compelled a grieving man with a borrowed spell, and you call it proof.”
> — Bookwyrm

*Bookwyrm attempts to undermine Alkrist's confession by citing the limitations of Kalan's own methodology.*

**Daz's keen investigation reveals that Bookwyrm, while appearing heartbroken over Alkrist's guilt, is actually curiously relieved—suggesting she knows more than she lets on.**

*Her collapse initially looks like grief, but Daz recognizes the emotion beneath it.*

> “I can't believe that there would be accomplices in the death of such a great man as Janussi, who is beloved by all.”
> — Bookwyrm

*Bookwyrm responds to the party's carefully limited disclosure with a conspicuously theatrical display of outrage.*

**Zalthir casually swipes the potion of flying from Alkrist's safe using Glabbagool while everyone else is distracted, and no one notices a thing.**

*Thorin summarizes the spirit of the maneuver: “Never miss an opportunity to grift, man.”*

> “Are we under house arrest?”
> — Zalthir

*Bookwyrm insists the confinement is purely protective, though Daz recognizes that she also wants the party kept from further investigation.*

> “I shall write, I shall do all kinds of new research on magical swords with the sentience and the beauty of Dawnbringer. I intend to make it an area of active research of mine.”
> — Daral

*Freshly cured, Daral immediately begins planning a scholarly career centered on the sentient sword who healed him.*

**Three helmed horrors smash through the windows and door of Deneir's Sanctum, launching a sudden and violent ambush on the party.**

*The attack begins just after Daral's enthusiastic lecture, while the group is still locked inside the chamber.*

> “That's not possible. How could they possibly… these creatures can't be here?”
> — Daral

*Daral reacts with academic disbelief as the helmed horrors invade the supposedly secure sanctum.*

> “Why is everybody piling up on me?”
> — Daz

*The constructs' relentless focus on Daz initially appears inexplicable.*

> “What you know about constructs is they don't have feelings, they have objectives.”
> — DM

*The horrors are not angry with Daz; their behavior reveals that he possesses something they were sent to obtain.*

**Mid-combat, the party realizes the mindless helmed horrors are specifically homing in on Daz and the inert, magically undetectable key he carries—raising the question of who sent them and how they knew.**

*Daz is left with only three hit points. When he passes the key to Gyrgum, the horrors redirect their attention toward its new bearer.*

> “Don't worry. I've got plenty of heal spells to get knocked out. I'll just heal you. You'll be back up in no time.”
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum encourages the critically wounded Daz to keep attacking, later adding that he also has Revivify.*

> “Now is not the time to worry about being knocked unconscious. Now is the time to cast magic missile.”
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum's battlefield reassurance is technically comforting, if not especially soothing.*

## Chapter 063

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/063-session-the-key-is-secured.md`; chapter 63; section Memorable Moments

**Daral's Detect Magic scroll reveals the party's gear blazing with magic — but the supposed high tower key remains completely dark, exposing Kalan's deception.**

> “This is not the key. This is not the key.”
> — Daral

*The party had been carrying what they believed was a real magical key, only to discover it was a mundane decoy all along. Daral had already insisted the true artifacts were “not some lowly, cheap-ass keys that you acquire at a locksmith.”*

> “I have given it now to someone who is not me.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch, as remembered by Gyrgum

*Kalan's strangely precise wording took on new meaning once the party discovered that he had transferred only a decoy, deliberately distancing himself from the apparent key while hiding the real one with Tadric.*

> “Bookwyrm is dead.”
> — Avowed messenger

*The Watcher's bell began tolling an alarm pattern moments after the fake key was exposed, and a breathless messenger arrived with news of another murder inside supposedly impregnable Candlekeep.*

> “This is Candlekeep. These crimes don't happen in Candlekeep.”
> — Avowed messenger

> “Well, reality disagrees.”
> — Gyrgum

*The horrified messenger struggled to reconcile Bookwyrm's murder with Candlekeep's reputation for absolute security.*

**The party discovers Bookwyrm's half-finished note reading “He is using the beast to—” beside her brutally torn-out throat, linking her murder to Sylvira's abyssal spawn son.**

*The gruesome scene and cryptic note reframe the entire investigation, pointing toward a monstrous new suspect and suggesting that someone else was directing the beast.*

> “Gadzooks. Someone is definitely after the keys.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*After learning that Bookwyrm's key was gone and that helmed horrors had attacked Daz for the apparent second key, Kalan could no longer dismiss the events as an isolated act of abyssal madness.*

> “I hereby deputize you as Watchers of Candlekeep.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*Kalan grandly deputizes the party moments after admitting he used them as unwitting decoys with a fake key. He offers a small badge and “a small honorarium,” then becomes exasperated that they are discussing compensation while Bookwyrm lies dead.*

> “Loose lips sink ships, dumbass.”
> — Thorin, to Fembris

*After Fembris admits A'lai Aivenmore was present when he reported the key transfer to Bookwyrm, Thorin warns him not to reveal that Tadric holds the genuine artifact.*

> “You have captured two of the pillars of the thrice fold proof.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch

> “You're making it sound harder than it is. I mean, why don't we just say three strikes are out?”
> — Thorin

*Even in an emergency, Kalan cannot resist translating the party's deductions into his cherished academic framework.*

> “Is this key for real this time? Are you just fucking with us again?”
> — Thorin

> “On my honor.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch

> “And the other one wasn't?”
> — Thorin

*Given Kalan's recent admission that the high tower key was a fake, even the key to the North Gallery shortcut receives intense scrutiny.*

> “Tadric, he's a loyal and he's alone and he does not know what he carries or what's coming for it.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*Kalan reveals that Tadric has been hidden away from the barracks with no understanding of either the key's significance or the creature already hunting him.*

**After Thorin's menacing blow frightens Moziqodo, Zalthir unleashes a relentless flurry of magically enhanced strikes on the demon spawn, stunning it completely and leaving it helpless for the rest of the party to finish off.**

*Going all-out immediately proved decisive, turning what should have been a brutal fight into a swift and overwhelming victory. Moziqodo never managed to take an action.*

**Thorin delivers a critical killing blow with Dawnbringer, slaying the demon spawn Moziqodo before it can flee or finish off Tadric, securing the real key to the high tower.**

> “You killed him. You killed him.”
> — Dungeon Master

*The party had feared the demon would escape with the key; instead, they concentrated their attacks so effectively that it collapsed before striking back.*

> “You're a radiant sword.”
> — Thorin

> “Thank you.”
> — Dawnbringer

*Thorin mistakes Dawnbringer for a fire weapon; told she deals radiant damage and is upset he did not notice, he makes amends, and she accepts the compliment. Both agree the incident will have to be covered in therapy.*

> “So far, we've done badly.”
> — Gyrgum, after being deputized

*The newly minted Watchers of Candlekeep respond to their promotion with characteristic confidence.*

## Chapter 065

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/065-session-08-03.md`; chapter 65; section Memorable Moments

**Daz’s fireball detonated successfully on the High Tower walkway—proving to all of Candlekeep that the library’s legendary magical wards had been broken.**

> **Daz:** “Everybody is going to realize that the Candlekeep is now in its underwear.”

The blast left the thug alive by a single hit point, but its larger consequence was immediate: screaming, panic, and ringing bells spread through Candlekeep. The Avowed also began taking notes and preparing monographs, prompting the observation:

> **GM:** “Because what do you do? What do you do in a situation like this? Why, conduct the experiments you've been waiting years to.”
>
> **Gyrgum:** “You take notes.”
>
> **GM:** “Exactly. You take notes. You take notes.”

**A'lai tried to redefine his surrender as an advantageous infiltration after the Zhentarim assassin arrived.**

> **A'lai Aivenmore:** “Took your time. They have had me since the landing.”
>
> **A'lai Aivenmore:** “It's not a loss. It's a position. I've been in the room. I know exactly who is carrying what.”

He then nodded toward Daz, causing the assassin to attack the wrong bearer of the sapphire.

**Thorin unleashed an action surge against the Zhentarim assassin—a menacing strike, a flurry of blows, and a devastating critical radiant hit—after the assassin knocked Daz unconscious.**

> **Thorin:** “Oh, I'm so badass.”
>
> **Thorin:** “Like, that's what you get for taking out one of my…”

Thorin’s full martial arsenal was on display. The assassin survived the critical radiant strike with five hit points, only for Thorin to finish him with Hill Strike.

**The party considered letting Glabbagool interrogate—or sample—A'lai.**

> **Glabbagool:** “I've never eaten a vowed wizard.”
>
> **Glabbagool:** “Hmm, do they dissolve like other people?”
>
> **A'lai Aivenmore:** “Keep that thing away from me.”
>
> **Thorin:** “I don't know. We should find out, Glabbagool.”

**Glabbagool received one of the real High Tower keys and visibly swelled with pride.**

> **GM:** “He gained a centimeter, you know, half a centimeter of height.”
>
> **GM:** “Little less pancakey.”
>
> **GM:** “A little less pancakey.”

**Manshoon’s simulacrum shattered the High Tower’s magical door with a wall of force, breaching Candlekeep’s inner sanctum despite the legendary wards—arriving alone and weakened, but present.**

The party’s success still mattered: Manshoon had been forced to expend enormous magical power to enter, could not bring his goons, and did not know the crucial information A'lai had withheld about the depository.

## Chapter 067

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/067-session-08-17.md`; chapter 67; section Memorable Moments

> “He's not looking for me. That's the part you don't understand. He sent a courier 11 years and the courier wear a glove. And I never saw the face. And I told myself that was discretion. It was not discretion. It was that I wasn't worth the face.”
> — A'lai Aivenmore

*A'lai realizes with horror that Manshoon never came for him. After eleven years as a pawn, he was not even worth being shown a face — much less worth killing.*

> “Not worth the spell slot.”
> — Gyrgum

*The party distills A'lai's existential collapse into the cruelest possible measurement of his value to Manshoon.*

> “We need to defeat Manshoon or the entire tower is at risk. We're in a hurry here and we've got to make compromises that we otherwise might not want to make.”
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum persuades Tadric to honor the bargain and place A'lai in the null magic prison.*

> “Look, and I founded an entire new philosophy.”
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum adds his academic credentials to the case for Tadric's cooperation, then promises they can publish a two-person monograph despite the uncertainties of anonymous submission.*

> “I could not trace the payments. I could only trace the patience.”
> — A'lai Aivenmore

*A'lai explains that his identification of Daz's mysterious patron rests not on a financial trail, but on the extraordinary patience and safety required to sustain the operation for eleven years.*

> “There is one house in Menzoberranzan that can afford to be patient. Baenre. It must be Baenre. No one else can afford to wait that long for nothing.”
> — A'lai Aivenmore

*A'lai's deduction about which drow house has been funding the operations around Daz for over a decade — offered with certainty, but reasoned from the patience of the payments rather than traced to a source.*

> “I do what I can for those that I can.”
> — Thorin

*Thorin's guarded response when Sylvira — still unaware that the party killed her son Moziqodo — thanks him for saving Tadric.*

> “Feed the quill of Alaundo the seer. Tread as many steps as he lived in years. Sprinkle dust of Mechanus on dormant gears.”
> — Thorin

*Thorin begins reading out the decoded instructions after cracking the substitution cipher.*

> “One last guardian of knowledge remains to verify the chosen Reader's claims, but those well versed in Candlekeep's lore may fearlessly pass the obsidian door.”
> — Thorin

*The final portion of the deciphered instructions, revealing both another guardian and an obsidian door ahead.*

> “I don't consider that a cheat because I could have done all that by hand. I didn't like it.”
> — Thorin

*Thorin defends his use of a cryptogram aid after solving the ancient cipher ahead of Candlekeep's assembled scholars.*

**Thorin deciphers the ancient cryptogram protecting the Vault of Secrets, leaving the assembled Candlekeep scholars stunned that a dwarf had solved a riddle guarding an ancient secret.**

*Scholars erupt in excited debate about whether this will be studied for years, particularly the part where Thorin cracked the cipher.*

> “Sorry, we already solved it.”
> — Gyrgum

*Kalan Strongbranch arrives huffing and puffing with an answer, only to discover that the party has finished the entire cipher.*

> “Yes, well, I'm relieved to see that. I'm relieved to see that.”
> — Kalan Strongbranch

*Kalan recovers from the stunned silence and searches for another way to make himself useful.*

**The party pours ink into the brass statue of Alaundo's ink pot, and the statue grinds forward on its dais to reveal a hidden staircase — a mechanism no scholar had ever triggered because defacing the statue was unthinkable.**

*Assembled scholars look on in horror at the realization that the answer was always this simple, and that centuries of reverence kept them from finding it.*

> “And nobody over all that time has put ink in the ink.”
> — Thorin

*Thorin identifies the obvious flaw in the ancient secret mechanism immediately after the statue moves.*

> “I'm not actually evil, so I'm going to start with ink.”
> — Gyrgum

*After the party debates whether “feed the quill” requires ink or blood.*

> “I mean, we don't kill librarians. They're our friends.”
> — Thorin

*Thorin rejects killing one of Spanner's modrons to produce Dust of Mechanus.*

> “We're game as long as you give us constructive feedback, such that we may better symbiotically serve our Zen cause.”
> — Zalthir

*Zalthir accepts Spanner's request to study him and Glabbagool in exchange for the modron tools.*

> “I think anytime you ask questions like this, it's followed by roll for initiative.”
> — Daz

*The GM repeatedly asks everyone to specify precisely where they are standing on the staircase.*

**A ten-foot section of the hidden staircase suddenly hinges open beneath the party's feet, sending Thorin plummeting into a vast, dark circular well as the session ends on a cliffhanger.**

*The party had spread across the stairs around the ninety-seventh step, with Daz above the hinge. Zalthir and Gyrgum caught themselves, and Daz seized the collapsing stairs with Telekinesis, but Thorin continued falling into the darkness.*

---

*Exported from GMAssistant on 2026-08-21*

## Chapter 068

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/068-session-08-24.md`; chapter 68; section Memorable Moments

> “Not this again!”  
> — Dawnbringer

*Thorin’s sentient weapon cries out in dismay as Thorin loses his footing and plummets into the darkness below the staircase trap.*

> “Wee!”  
> — Glabbagool

*Glabbagool reacts to the terrifying fall with considerably more enthusiasm than Dawnbringer.*

> “One should always be Zen.”  
> — Glabbagool

*Glabbagool offers calm philosophical advice while Thorin, Zalthir, and the ooze remain suspended together in a mid-air embrace.*

> “Is this too much hugging?”  
> — Glabbagool

> “There are worse things, Glabbagool.”  
> — Zalthir

*Zalthir catches Thorin roughly five hundred feet into the fall, creating a crowded airborne rescue.*

> “Hey, are you guys okay down there?”  
> — Gyrgum

> “So far.”  
> — Zalthir

> “I’m still kicking.”  
> — Thorin

*The party checks on Thorin and Zalthir from the trapped staircase far above.*

> “I mean, we’re already halfway down. We could just go scout a bit.”  
> — Zalthir

*Having caught Thorin after a five-hundred-foot plunge, Zalthir decides that returning to the stairs would be less efficient than finishing the descent.*

> “Look, if all your friends jumped off the stairs, would you?”  
> — Gyrgum

> “Absolutely.”  
> — Gyrgum

*After learning about the feather-fall zone, the rest of the party prepares to leap voluntarily into the shaft.*

> “I intend to write a very long monograph.”  
> — Edvaldo Sedanur

*The Candlekeep historian agrees to jump into a previously unknown subterranean region beneath the library.*

**Thorin falls through the hinged staircase trap and plummets into the darkness, while Zalthir dives after him and catches him mid-air five hundred feet below, with Glabbagool philosophically wondering if there is too much hugging.**

*Daz is still holding the broken stairs in place with telekinesis as Thorin falls and Zalthir dives after him.*

> “Halt, seekers. Before you consult the mysteries stored herein, you must prove yourselves a worthy keeper of secrets. Three questions you must answer before I let you pass.”  
> — Iron Owlbear Guardian

*The tiny winged statue rises from its pedestal and subjects the party to a formal trial of Candlekeep lore.*

> “It’s been a while. It’s been a while. It’s been a while. But don’t you worry. If you fail, I will tear you to shreds and destroy you.”  
> — Iron Owlbear Guardian

*The guardian recovers from accidentally reading from its answer key and enthusiastically recalls its intended purpose.*

> “I’ve been waiting a long time to do this. This is something I have trained for in my head.”  
> — Iron Owlbear Guardian

*The guardian reveals how eagerly it has anticipated someone failing its three questions.*

> “I founded a new school that holds that there are certain fixed points among which we may make our own paths.”  
> — Gyrgum

> “Why, wisdom, wisdom truly flows from Bahamut into you, cleric of Bahamut.”  
> — Miirym

*Gyrgum answers the dragon’s theological question and wins the ancient guardian’s admiration.*

> “I was bound to be witness, not weapon. If I could have stopped him, I would have stopped him at dawn.”  
> — Miirym

*The spectral dragon explains why she cannot personally join the battle against Manshoon.*

> “Well chosen. Well chosen. Well chosen.”  
> — Miirym

*The ancient dragon approves after the party selects the boon of extending the anti-magic field to neutralize Manshoon’s spellcasting in the coming battle.*

> “By all means, browse. The trick is leaving with what you came for. Did you come for the book or did you come for me?”  
> — Manshoon

*The legendary wizard barely looks up from his book as the party enters the secret vault.*

> “Indeed, we came for you. We did not know of the books.”  
> — Gyrgum

> “The books are but a bonus.”  
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum clarifies the party’s priorities upon discovering Candlekeep’s collection of forbidden magical knowledge.*

**Gyrgum persuades the legendary and dangerous wizard Manshoon to voluntarily relocate their duel to a lower chamber—to protect the priceless books from being damaged in the fight.**

*With complete earnestness, Gyrgum appeals to Manshoon’s respect for rare knowledge. Daz calls for an inspiration point, Gyrgum rolls exactly the required 22, and the appeal works.*

> “There’s a lot of valuable books in this room. It would be a shame to damage any of them. What if we moved into the other room so they’d be safer?”  
> — Gyrgum

> “Yes, you’re correct. We should not damage these books. They are of priceless in origin.”  
> — Manshoon

*The two sides pause their imminent battle to agree upon responsible treatment of the collection.*

> “Let’s all just leave them here for now. We can come back after we kill you.”  
> — Gyrgum

> “Or I killed you.”  
> — Manshoon

> “Or, yes. Yes, I admit it is a possibility.”  
> — Gyrgum

*The cleric and wizard politely acknowledge both possible outcomes of the coming duel.*

**The spectral silver dragon, lava flowing through her ethereal form, reveals that Manshoon has entered her keep and is still inside.**

*The party meets Candlekeep’s dragon guardian in the lava cavern and learns that they are already too late to stop Manshoon’s entry.*

## Chapter 069

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/069-session-one-manshoon-dead-at-least-one-or-two-more-to-go.md`; chapter 69; section Memorable Moments

**The defeated Manshoon instantly dissolves into a pile of melting snow, revealing the entire battle was fought against a simulacrum—a construct of ice and snow—and that at least one more version waits within the tower.**

> “The Great Manshoon, the great Manshoon is dead, except he turns into snow.”
> — DM

*The party's hard-won victory turns to disbelief as the supposed archmage reverts to snow and melts instantly in the heat of the magma chamber.*

> “Snow.”
> — Daz

*Daz provides the party's succinct first response to Manshoon's transformation.*

> “Jeez, do we have to kill the real one?”
> — Gyrgum

*The group immediately confronts the possibility that defeating this duplicate was only the beginning.*

> “This was much better than the last one. The last one didn't even stop to say hello.”
> — The Flying Iron Owlbear

*The winged owlbear provides cheerful color commentary on the party's brutal combat performance immediately after they destroy the Manshoon simulacrum.*

> “I would rather not do this the expensive way. I've been polite for 6 hours, and it has become something of a habit.”
> — Manshoon (Simulacrum)

*The second Manshoon simulacrum, having waited alone for hours in a chamber of deadly warded books, calmly proposes a bargain to the party.*

> “Here's what I propose, and I would like you to notice that it is not a threat.”
> — Manshoon (Simulacrum)

*While holding access to the party's desired prophecies over their heads, Manshoon takes pains to characterize his ultimatum as something other than a threat.*

> “If you want the dwarf's prophecy, I want a door. These are not the same object. Your cleric says one word, the door opens. You take the tray, I take the book.”
> — Manshoon (Simulacrum)

*The second simulacrum lays out the exact terms of his bargain.*

> “To the last door I gave no lock, for a lock is a promise that a thing can be stolen. I gave it a question instead.”
> — Alaundo

*A recorded echo of the legendary seer Alaundo's voice speaks the riddle of the Obsidian Door, leaving the party puzzling over its meaning.*

*Editorial note: “I gave it a question instead” is restored from the selected session prep with GM approval, not transcribed verbatim from ASR.*

**Thorin considers throwing the sentient sword Dawnbringer as a ranged weapon, prompting a silent moment of mutual judgment between dwarf and blade before he opts for a dart instead.**

> “I'm a sword, not a spear.”
> — Dawnbringer

*The DM summarizes the dignified sentient blade's likely objection to being hurled across the room.*

> “Thorin looks at Dawnbringer, Dawnbringer looks at Thorin, there's a little bit of moment… there's a moment there about, you know, decisions, and what kinds of decisions are the right decisions, and, you know, team… team spirit, and that kind of thing.”
> — DM

*Immobilized and nearly out of ranged options, Thorin weighs necessity against Dawnbringer's dignity before throwing a dart instead.*

> “A dwarf will return to the Hall of Stone with the names of demons in her mouth.”
> — Disembodied Voice

*After combat and Edvaldo's binding, a magical voice speaks a prophecy from above. The returning dwarf appears to be Eldeth, the giant-raised dwarf is Thorin, and the shadow-walker is Zalthir.*

> “Don't worry, if you die, I can bring you back.”
> — Gyrgum

*Cheerfully shouted to the badly wounded researcher Edvaldo, whose survival was already defying simple arithmetic.*

> “Tell me the history of Candlekeep.”
> — Zalthir

*Zalthir responds to Edvaldo's claim of innocence with an improvised identity test while holding him above a lethal magma chamber.*

> “Well, we know you're not an LLM now.”
> — Ben Pfaff, out of character

*Ben jokes at the GM's hesitation when asked to improvise Candlekeep history, before Edvaldo's fluent account.*

> “But by the way, for future reference, Glabbagool, if there's an instant death trap you see that I forget about, feel free to remind me.”
> — Zalthir

*After Glabbagool wonders why Manshoon was not simply dropped into the lava, Zalthir establishes a new standing tactical instruction.*

> “But as long as I get to be second author on the paper about the combat.”
> — Edvaldo

*Edvaldo abruptly revises his wish to be left behind once academic credit enters the negotiation.*

> “There must be a whole factory of these things.”
> — Gyrgum

*After learning that Manshoon has been dead for 170 years and detecting another simulacrum upstairs, Gyrgum reaches the obvious conclusion.*

> “Books!”
> — Edvaldo

*The bound scholar's first desperate answer to the question of what is consumed to make light.*

> “Candles. Candles are what's consumed to make light. You're in Candlekeep. How obvious could it be?”
> — Edvaldo

*Edvaldo quickly revises his answer while insisting that the riddle is embarrassingly straightforward.*

## Chapter 070

### Memorable Moments

- Provenance: `/home/kostadis/out-of-the-abyss/out-of-the-abyss/docs/summaries/070-session-untitled.md`; chapter 70; section Memorable Moments

**Zalthir interrupts Manshoon's monologue with a blunt declaration of intent.**

> “Before you say, before you say it, I want to punch him.”
> — Zalthir

*The punch becomes a grapple, instantly turning Manshoon's carefully controlled negotiation into chaos.*

**Zalthir uses his extraordinary monk speed to carry the screaming Manshoon simulacrum out of the tower and hurl him into the lava pit.**

> “Yeah, no, I let him go. I toss him over the edge.”
> — Zalthir

*Even the possibility that Manshoon's death might destroy the prophecy crystals does not stop Zalthir from accepting the risk.*

> “A candle.”
> — Gyrgum

*Gyrgum, exhausted by the prolonged standoff with two Manshoon simulacra and a doppelganger, flatly speaks the answer to Alaundo's grand riddle and opens the ancient door.*

> “Oh, it worked. First of all, I'm surprised. I mean, that's the dumbest answer I can ever think of.”
> — Gyrgum

*The enchantment cared less about the cleverness of the answer than the sincerity with which a Reader gave it.*

**Daz identifies the book in the inner chamber as the Book of Vile Darkness, a sentient artifact of cosmic evil written or purportedly written by the Archlich Vecna.**

> “I'm not going to touch it just yet because I kind of know how bad that is.”
> — Daz

*The party fought through two Manshoon simulacra and a doppelganger to reach the room, only to find one of the most dangerous artifacts in existence.*

**The sentient Book of Vile Darkness speaks to Daz, offering him a choice.**

> “Do you want to know something about yourself or do you want to know something about how to defeat the demons?”
> — The Book of Vile Darkness

*Daz had used Mage Hand to avoid touching the book and resisted its corrupting influence before its sentience revealed itself.*

**Daz chooses knowledge of the demons over knowledge of himself.**

> “I think he would ask about the demons because I think Daz believes that knowing yourself is something that you have to do on your own.”
> — Daz

*Faced with an offer of personal revelation, Daz instead chooses information relevant to the demonic crisis, though he knows the book's narrator cannot be trusted.*

**Edvaldo, already exposed as more than an ordinary Reader, dramatically reveals his doppelganger nature at the worst possible moment—just as Zalthir thinks he has the situation under control.**

> “If you kill him, this place will blow up.”
> — Edvaldo

*The party had assumed the suspicious prisoner was secured, only for him to escape by changing shape and appear at the top of the stairs with a warning about Manshoon's explosive fail-safe.*

**Edvaldo smashes two of Alaundo's prophecy crystals in a panic, threatening to destroy the rest unless the party opens the door for his master.**

> “All you had to do was give us the damn book.”
> — Edvaldo

> “Right. Yeah, we're not doing that.”
> — Zalthir

*Desperate and terrified after watching Manshoon get thrown into lava, Edvaldo resorts to destroying irreplaceable prophecies as leverage, but the party remains unmoved.*

**The party acknowledges just how many permanent choices they made in a single confrontation.**

> “We made a few irrevocable choices in this one.”
> — Gyrgum

> “Yes, we threw Manshoon into the pit, and I opened the door.”
> — Gyrgum

*By the end of the session, one Manshoon was in the lava, two prophecies had been destroyed, the last door stood open, and Daz was reading the Book of Vile Darkness.*
